Interactive reader · Subject guide and indexes

The Immense Ocean — Āl ʿImrān

Arabic: Cairo Ruslān 1419/1999; English: Translation Revision XVI / XVIII. Arabic is partially vocalized where the source has vowel marks. Text aligned by commentary unit; topic subdivisions are modern editorial aids.

Sūrah introduction · Sūrat Āl ʿImrān — The Family of ʿImrān

Cairo I:321

Exposition · Rebuke, revelation and the three communities

It is Medinan. Its verses number two hundred; it has also been said, one hundred and eighty-seven. Its words number three thousand four hundred and eighty.[A001] Its connection with the preceding sūrah is His saying near its beginning, “Those who disbelieve in God’s signs …”: this is, as it were, a completion and explanation of “give us victory over the unbelieving people.” Its contents direct reproach towards three groups: the Christians, for their excess concerning Jesus, peace be upon him, and their refusal to enter Islam—it was on their account that the sūrah was revealed, namely the Christians of Najrān; the Jews, for their failure to follow the Prophet, blessings and peace be upon him; and the Muslims, for the loss of resolve that befell them on the day of Uḥud.[A002] For this reason He opens the sūrah by mentioning the three books: had they fulfilled their obligations towards them, no reproach would have been directed at them. Thus He says:

سورة آل عمران مدنية. وآياتها: مائتان، وقيل: مائة وسبع وثمانون. وكلماتها: ثلاثة آلاف وأربعمائة وثمانون كلمة، ومناسبتها لما قبلها: قوله تعالى فى أولها: إِنَّ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا بِآياتِ اللَّهِ ... إلخ، فكأنه تتميم لقوله، فَانْصُرْنا عَلَى الْقَوْمِ الْكافِرِينَ، وتفسير له. ومضمنها: توجيه العتاب لثلاث طوائف: للنصارى لغلوهم في عيسى عليه السلام، ولامتناعهم من الدخول فى الإسلام، وبسببهم نزلت السورة، أعنى نصارى نجران، ولليهود لتفريطهم فى اتباع النبي- عليه الصلاة والسلام- وللمسلمين لما وقع لهم من الفشل يوم أحد، ولذلك افتتح السورة بذكر الكتب الثلاثة، إذ لو قاموا بحقوقها ما توجه لهم عتاب، فقال:

3:1–3 · 3:1–3 · The Living, the Self-Subsisting and the revealed books

Cairo I:321

1. Alif. Lām. Mīm. 2. God—there is no god but He, the Living, the Self-Subsisting. 3. He has sent down upon you the Book in truth, confirming what came before it; and He sent down the Torah and the Gospel …

Language · The opening letters

I say: all the sūrahs’ opening letters are pronounced in pause and have no syntactic inflection (الإعراب), since that which would occasion and require it is absent. They are therefore paused upon with sukūn, as when one enumerates “one, two.” In the well-known recitation, the mīm here is given fatḥa only because the vowel of the following hamza is transferred to it. Consult al-Bayḍāwī.[A003] Ibn ʿAbbās—God be pleased with him—said: “Alif is His favours (آلاؤه), lām His gentleness (لطفه), and mīm His dominion (ملكه).” I say: perhaps each letter points to one of the groups to whom the reproach is directed. The favours are for those Christians who embraced Islam; the gentleness for those Jews who embraced it; and dominion for the Companions who embraced Islam—God’s good pleasure be upon them—for God gave them possession of the earth’s east and west. And God, exalted is He, knows best.

Exposition · The Qurʾān confirms the earlier revelation

The Real, glorious is His majesty, says: O exalted sovereign, O magnified Messenger, convey to your people that God is One in His dominion. There is no deity beside Him, nor does He love that another be worshipped with Him. None deserves worship but the Living, the Self-Subsisting (الحي القيوم), whom intellects and the apprehensions of understanding cannot encompass. He attends to His servants’ affairs and disposes of them according to His will. He has left them without excuse through the messengers’ tongues and sent down books as clarification for those seeking guidance. Thus “He has sent down upon you the Book,” in instalments over twenty years,[A004] “in truth”: clothed in truth, so that “falsehood cannot come to it from before it or behind it” [41:42]; or clothed in proofs that repel every falsehood; or in justice, through which the oppression of every deviating person is removed. It is “confirming” the divine books that preceded it, agreeing with their narratives and reports, and so bearing witness to their truth and rectitude.

“And He sent down the Torah and the Gospel …”

الم (1) اللَّهُ لآَ إِلهَ إِلاَّ هُوَ الْحَيُّ الْقَيُّومُ (2) نَزَّلَ عَلَيْكَ الْكِتابَ بِالْحَقِّ مُصَدِّقاً لِما بَيْنَ يَدَيْهِ وَأَنْزَلَ التَّوْراةَ وَالْإِنْجِيلَ (3)

3:4 · 3:4 · Guidance, discernment and the warning to unbelievers

Cairo I:321–322

4. … before, as guidance for humankind; and He sent down the Criterion. Those who disbelieve in God’s signs will have a severe punishment. God is Mighty, Possessed of requital.

Exposition · The Criterion and the earlier books

He sent them down before it, as guidance for those people charged with following them; or for all, on the principle that the Sacred Law of those before us is law for us, the community of Islam. He then sealed revelation by sending down “the Criterion” (الفرقان), charging humans and jinn with believing in it. Through it He distinguished truth from falsehood and dispelled the darkness of every unbeliever and ignorant person. He mentioned it before the other books because of its immense honour, and mentioned it last because it was the last to descend. And God, exalted is He, knows best.

Spiritual · Understanding the mysteries of revelation

When the Real, glorious is His majesty, wished to point to the unity of the Essence and the manifestation of the lights of the Attributes, He first set forth symbols and allusions understood only by one who has dived into the depths of the ocean of the Essence and been submerged in the current of the Attributes. Through his contemplation he brings out precious jewels of knowledge and subtle understandings at which minds stand bewildered and for which the tongue’s expression fails. Then he understands the subtleties of the symbols and the secrets of the allusions, and gains insight into the mysteries of the Essence and lights of the Attributes. He understands the mysteries of the heavenly books and the divinely bestowed sciences (العلوم اللدنية) and lordly gifts they contain. The lights of discernment shine in his heart until he rises to the realization of those possessed of witnessing and direct vision. May God make us among them through His gracious favour and generosity.

Transition ·

He then threatens those who disbelieve in the Criterion after the radiance of the proof has become clear, saying:

Qurʾānic continuation · 3:4b
إِنَّ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا بِآياتِ اللَّهِ لَهُمْ عَذابٌ شَدِيدٌ وَاللَّهُ عَزِيزٌ ذُو انْتِقامٍ
Those who disbelieve in God’s signs will have a severe punishment. God is Mighty, Possessed of requital.

Language · Requital

I say: intiqām and niqma (الانتقام والنقمة) mean the punishment of an offender. The verb is naqima or naqama, with kasra or fatḥa on the qāf.

Exposition · Might, punishment and gentleness

The Real, glorious is His majesty, says: “Those who disbelieve in God’s signs,” sent down to His Prophet or to all His prophets, or in the signs indicating His oneness, “will have a severe punishment” on the day when promise and warning are seen to take effect. God will then exact requital from the offenders and turn tenderly towards His believing servants. For “God is Mighty”: no vanquisher overcomes Him and no fugitive escapes Him. He is possessed of great requital and abundant gentleness. May God show gentleness to us and to all Muslims. Amen.

Spiritual · The loss of the blessing of God’s friends

The appearance of God’s friends is a gracious manifestation of God’s signs. Whoever rejects them is deprived of their blessing, remaining in the torment of the veil and an ill reckoning. Requital and affliction become manifest upon him when God raises those brought near into the highest heights, while the heedless remain with the ordinary Muslims. “That is the Day of Mutual Loss and Gain” [64:9]. And God, exalted is He, knows best.

Transition ·

Having described Himself by oneness, life and self-subsistence—which entail absolute independence—the Real, glorious is His majesty, also describes Himself by encompassing knowledge and effectual power, saying:

مِنْ قَبْلُ هُدىً لِلنَّاسِ وَأَنْزَلَ الْفُرْقانَ ... قلت: فواتح السور كلها موقوفة خالية عن الإعراب لفقدان مُوجبه ومقتضيه، فيوقف عليها بالسكون، كقولهم: واحد، اثنان. وإنما فَتَحَ الميم هنا في القراءة المشهورة لإلقاء حركة الهمزة عليها. انظر البيضاوي. قال ابن عباس رضي الله عنه: (الألف آلاؤه، واللام لطفه، والميم مُلكه) . قلت: ولعلَّ كل حرف يشير إلى فرقة ممن توجَّه العتاب إليهم، فالآلاء لِمنْ أسلم من النصارى، واللطف لمن أسلم من اليهود، والملك لمن أسلم من الصحابة- رضوان الله عليهم-، فقد ملكهم الله مشارق الأرض ومغاربها. والله تعالى أعلم. يقول الحق جل جلاله: أيها الملك المُعظَّم، والرسول المفخم، بلِّغ قومك أن الله واحد في ملكه، ليس معه إله، ولا يُحب أن يُعبد معه سواه إذ لا يستحق أن يعبد إلا الحيّ القيّوم، الذي تعجز عن إدراكه العقولُ ومدارك الفهوم، قائم بأمر عباده، متصرف فيهم، على وفق مراده، فأعذر إليهم على ألسنة المرسلين، وأنزل عليهم الكتب بياناً للمسترشدين، فنزَّل عَلَيْكَ الْكِتابَ مُنَجّماً في عشرين سنة، متلبساً بِالْحَقِّ، حتى لا يَأْتِيهِ الْباطِلُ مِنْ بَيْنِ يَدَيْهِ وَلا مِنْ خَلْفِهِ، أو متلبساً بالحجج التي تدفع كل باطل، أو بالعدل حتى ينتفي به جَوْر كل مائل، مُصَدِّقاً لما تقدم قبله من الكتب الإلهية إذ هو موافق لما فيها من القصص والأخبار، فكان شاهداً عليها بالصحة والإبرار. وَأَنْزَلَ التَّوْراةَ وَالْإِنْجِيلَ من قبله هادياً لمن كلف باتباعهما من الأنام، أو للجميع، إذا كان شرعُ منْ قبلنا شرعاً لنا- معشر أهل الإسلام-، ثم ختم الوحي بإنزال الْفُرْقانَ، وكلّف بالإيمان به الإنس والجان، فرَّق به بين الحق والباطل، واندفع به ظلمة كل كافر وجاهل وقدَّم ذكره على الكتب لعظم شرفه، وختم به آخراً لتأخر نزوله. والله تعالى أعلم. الإشارة: لمّا أراد الحق جل جلاله أن يشير إلى وحدة الذات وظهور أنوار الصفات، قَدَّم قبل ذلك رموزاً وإشارات، لا يفهمها إلا من غاص في قاموس بحر الذات، وغرق في تيار الصفات، فيستخرج بفكرته من يواقيت العلوم وغوامض الفهوم، ما تحار فيه الأذهان، وتكِلُّ عنه عبارةُ اللسان، فحينئذ يفهم دقائق الرموز وأسرار الإشارات، ويطلع على أسرار الذات وأنوار الصفات، ويفهم أسرار الكتب السماوية، وما احتوت عليه من العلوم اللدنية، والمواهب الربانية، ويشرق في قلبه أنوار الفرقان، حتى يرتقي إلى تحقيق أهل الشهود والعيان. جعلنا الله منهم بمنِّه وكرمه. ثم هدد من كفر بالفرقان، بعد وضوح سواطع البرهان، فقال: ... إِنَّ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا بِآياتِ اللَّهِ لَهُمْ عَذابٌ شَدِيدٌ وَاللَّهُ عَزِيزٌ ذُو انْتِقامٍ (4) قلت: الانتقام والنقمة: عقوبة المجرم. وفعله: نقم بكسر القاف وفتحها. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: إِنَّ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا بِآياتِ اللَّهِ المنزلة على نبيه أو على سائر أنبيائه، أو الآيات الدالة على وحدانيته، لَهُمْ عَذابٌ شَدِيدٌ يوم يظهر نفوذ الوعد والوعيد، فينتقم الله فيه من المجرمين، ويتعطف على عباده المؤمنين، فإن اللَّهُ عَزِيزٌ لا يغلبه غالب، ولا يفُوته هارب، ذُو انْتِقامٍ كبير ولطف كثير. لطف الله بنا وبجميع المسلمين. آمين. الإشارة: ظهور أولياء الله لطف من آيات الله، فمن كفر بهم حُرم بركتهم، وبقي في عذاب الحجاب وسوء الحساب، تظهر عليه النقمة والمحنة، حين يرفع الله المقربين في أعلى عليين، ويكون الغافلون مع عوام المسلمين، (ذلك يوم التغابن) . والله تعالى أعلم. ولما وصف الحق جل جلاله نفسه بالوحدانية والحياة والقيومية المقتضية للغنى المطلق، وصف نفسه أيضا بالعلم المحيط والقدرة النافذة، فقال:

3:5–6 · 3:5–6 · Knowledge, power and formation in the womb

Cairo I:322–323

5. Nothing in the earth or in heaven is hidden from God. 6. He it is who forms you in the wombs as He wills. There is no god but He, the Mighty, the Wise.

Exposition · Nothing escapes His knowledge or power

The Real, glorious is His majesty, says: “Nothing is hidden from God” concerning His creatures: belief or unbelief, obedience or disobedience. His knowledge encompasses whatever is in the high heavens and the lower earths, universal or particular, sensible or intelligible. He knows the number of pebbles and grains of sand, the measures of the waters and the weights of the mountains. He knows the occurrences of inward consciousness and the stirrings of thoughts, with beginningless, eternal knowledge. His power is effectual and His wisdom consummate. By His power He formed the drops in the wombs however He willed, with deficiency or completeness, perfected them through His wisdom, and brought them forth to the sustenance He had made available to them. Glory to the Disposer who is Knowing, Mighty and Wise! Nothing incapacitates Him and nothing falls outside the compass of His knowledge. There is no existent besides Him, and we worship none but Him.[A005] Success is through God, who guides to the even path.

Spiritual · Trust in the One who first formed you

Whoever realizes that God is One in His dominion, without associate in His Essence, Attributes or acts; that He encompasses him in knowledge, hearing and sight; and that His command is between kāf and nūn—“when He wills a thing, He says to it, ‘Be,’ and it is” [compare 36:82]—how could he complain to anyone else of what descends upon him from Him? How could he raise his needs to other than his Master? How could he nurse anxiety, when his Master does not forget to bestow His goodness upon him? He who cared for you in the darkness of the inward parts, formed you in the wombs as He willed, and gave you all you ask and wish: how would He forget to show you His goodness and beneficence? How would He place you outside the compass of His gentleness and favour? Concerning this, the tongue of Reality (لسان الحقيقة) says:

Remember My kindness to you when you were a drop;
do not forget My forming your person in the womb.
Trust Me in all your affairs:
I shall suffice you against what is feared and dreaded.
Surrender the affair to Me, and know that I
dispose My decrees and do whatever I will.

Transition ·

He then describes His book, the Criterion, as containing what is decisive and clearly explained, and what is ambiguous, known only to God and to those firmly grounded among the people of gnosis, saying:

إِنَّ اللَّهَ لا يَخْفى عَلَيْهِ شَيْءٌ فِي الْأَرْضِ وَلا فِي السَّماءِ (5) هُوَ الَّذِي يُصَوِّرُكُمْ فِي الْأَرْحامِ كَيْفَ يَشاءُ لاَ إِلهَ إِلاَّ هُوَ الْعَزِيزُ الْحَكِيمُ (6) يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: إِنَّ اللَّهَ لا يَخْفى عَلَيْهِ شَيْءٌ من أمر خلقه، إيماناً أو كفراناً، طاعة أو عصياناً، أحاط علمه بما فى السموات العلي وما في الأرضين السفلى، كليّاً كان أو جزئيّاً، حسيّاً أو معنوياً، يعلم عدد الحصى والرمال، ومكاييل المياه ومثاقيل الجبال، ويعلم حوادث الضمائر، وهواجس الخواطر، بعلم قديم أزلي، وله قدرة نافذة، وحكمة بالغة، فبقدرته صَوَّرَ النُّطَف في الأرحام كيف شاء سبحانه من نقص أو تمام، وأتقنها بحكمته، وأبرزها إلى ما يَسَّرَ لها من رزقه، سبحانه من مدبر عليم، عزيز حكيم، لا يعجزه شيء، ولا يخرج عن دائرة علمه شيء، لا موجود سواه، ولا نعبد إلا إياه، وبالله التوفيق، وهو الهادي إلى سواء الطريق. الإشارة: مَنْ تحقق أن الله واحد في ملكه، لا شريك له في ذاته ولا في صفاته ولا أفعاله، وأنه أحاط به علماً وسمعاً وبصراً، وأن أمره بين الكاف والنون، (إِذَآ أَرَادَ شَيْئاً أَن يقولَ له كُن فيكون) - كيف يشكو ما نزل به منه إلى أحد سواه؟ أم كيف يرفع حوائجه إلى غير مولاه؟ أم كيف يعول هما، وسيدُه من خيره لا ينساه؟ من دبرك في ظلمة الأحشاء، وصوَّرك في الأرحام كيف يشاء، وآتاك كل ما تسأل وتشاء، كيف يَنْساكَ من بره وإحسانه؟ أم كيف يخرجك عن دائرة لطفه وامتنانه؟ وفي ذلك يقول لسان الحقيقة: تَذَكَّر جَمِيلِي فِيكَ إِذْ كُنْتَ نُطْفَةً ... وَلا تَنْسَ تَصْوِيرِي لشَخْصِكَ في الْحَشا وَكُنْ وَاثِقاً بِي في أُمُورِكَ كُلِّها ... سأَكْفِيكَ مِنْهَا ما يُخافُ ويُخْتَشَى وَسَلِّمْ ليّ الأمْرَ واعْلَمْ بأنني ... أُصَرِّفُ أحْكَامِي وأَفْعَلُ ما أشا ثم وصف كتابه الفرقان بأنه مشتمل على ما هو محكم واضح البيان، وعلى ما هو متشابه لا يعلمه إلا الله، والراسخون من أهل العرفان، فقال:

3:7–9 · 3:7–9 · The decisive, the ambiguous and those firmly grounded in knowledge

Cairo I:323–326

7. He it is who sent down upon you the Book. In it are decisive verses: they are the foundation of the Book; and others are ambiguous. Those in whose hearts is deviation follow what is ambiguous in it, seeking discord and seeking its interpretation. None knows its interpretation but God and those firmly grounded in knowledge, saying, “We believe in it; all is from our Lord.” None takes heed but those possessed of understanding. 8. “Our Lord, do not cause our hearts to deviate after You have guided us. Grant us mercy from Your presence: You are indeed the Bestower. 9. Our Lord, You will gather humankind for a day of which there is no doubt. God does not fail the promise.”

Language · Where the sentence ends

I say: “In it” (منه) is a preceding predicate (خبر مقدم), and “verses” is the subject (مبتدأ); one therefore pauses at “the Book.” It has also been said that “in it” qualifies “a book,” but that is remote. Ibn al-Subkī says: “The decisive (المحكم) is that whose meaning is clear; the ambiguous (المتشابه) is that whose knowledge God reserves to Himself, although He may acquaint some of His chosen ones with it.”[A006] “They are the foundation of the Book” is a sentence. Normally the predicate should agree in number, giving “foundations” (أمهات). It is singular here either with the sense “each one [is a foundation],” or because the whole is treated as one verse. Zaygh (الزيغ) is deviation from truth. “Those firmly grounded in knowledge” (الراسخون في العلم) is coordinated with “God”; or it begins a new sentence, if the ambiguous is understood as that whose knowledge God reserves to Himself, such as how long the world will endure and when the Hour will arise, or that whose apparent meaning decisive proof shows not to be intended. So says al-Bayḍāwī. “When You guided us” (إذ هديتنا) is an adverbial expression in the genitive through annexation, construed as a verbal noun: “after Your guidance of us.”[A007]

Exposition · The labour and rank of interpretation

The Real, glorious is His majesty, says: the One who alone possesses oneness and self-subsistence, from whom nothing in the higher or lower world is hidden, “is He who sent down upon you the clear Book.” Some of it consists of “decisive verses,” clear in meaning, without ambiguity or indeterminacy. “They are the foundation of the Book”: its root, to which the rest is referred. Some consists of “other ambiguous verses,” admitting possibilities: their intent is not clear, because of indeterminacy or an apparently conflicting sense, without examination and sound reflection. This is so that the excellence of discerning scholars may appear, and their eagerness to exert themselves in pondering these verses and acquiring the sciences necessary to derive their intent may increase. Through this, through tiring their minds in extracting their meanings and reconciling them with the decisive verses, they attain the highest degrees and loftiest stations. In Nawādir al-uṣūl, in discussing the ambiguous, its author divides it into two kinds. One is that whose knowledge is withheld except from the elect, such as the knowledge of the sūrahs’ opening letters. The other is that which none of the messengers, nor anyone beneath them, has attained: the mystery of predestination (سر القدر). This is not compatible with their servanthood: were it unveiled, servanthood would be impaired. He therefore concealed it from the messengers and angels, since they are in servanthood. When servanthood passes away, they will be able to bear it—that is, the mysteries of predestination. End of quotation.[A008] Sahl’s saying points to something of this kind: “Divinity has a mystery which, if disclosed, would nullify prophethood; prophethood has a mystery which, if disclosed, would nullify knowledge; and knowledge has a mystery which, if disclosed, would nullify the rulings.” End of quotation. I say: it follows that the Mighty Book contains the decisive and the ambiguous. As for His words, “A Book whose verses have been made firm” [11:1], they mean that the verses are preserved from corrupt meaning and feeble expression. His words “a mutually resembling Book” [39:23] mean that its parts resemble one another in soundness of meaning and strength of expression.

Exposition · Deviation and steadfast understanding

People’s relation to the ambiguous is of two kinds. “Those in whose hearts is deviation”—doubt or a turning from truth, such as innovators and their like—“follow what is ambiguous in it.” They attach themselves to its apparent sense, or to a false interpretation, “seeking discord”: seeking to turn people from their religion through doubt and deception, setting the ambiguous against the decisive. And “seeking its interpretation” according to their desires, so that it agrees with their innovation.

It is related from ʿĀʾisha—God be pleased with her—that the Prophet, blessings and peace be upon him, recited this verse and said: “When you see those who ask about what is ambiguous in it and dispute concerning it, they are those whom God meant. Beware of them, and do not sit with them.”[A009] “None knows its interpretation” in reality “but God,” exalted is He. Yet He may acquaint some of His specially chosen friends with it: they are “those firmly grounded,” established “in knowledge,” the gnostics of God, the people of effacement and subsistence (الفناء والبقاء), the people of the distinctive realization of unity. He has acquainted them with the mysteries of His unseen, so that nothing ambiguous remains for them in the Book or the Sunna. They say, “We believe in it,” affirming that it is His speech. “All is from our Lord,” decisive and ambiguous alike, and we have understood His intention in both. These are those possessed of understanding, whom He praises by saying, “None takes heed but those possessed of understanding”: hearts purified of the darkness of desire and the haze of sense-perception.[A010] The Prophet, blessings and peace be upon him, was asked who the firmly grounded in knowledge were. He replied: “One whose oath proves true, whose tongue is truthful, whose heart is upright, and whose belly and private parts are chaste: that is one firmly grounded in knowledge.”[A011] Nāfiʿ ibn Yazīd said: “Those firmly grounded in knowledge are those humble before God, abasing themselves in seeking God’s good pleasure. They do not exalt themselves against those above them, nor despise those beneath them.” End of quotation. It has also been said: one firmly grounded in knowledge is one in whom four things are found—godfearingness between himself and God, humility between himself and creatures, renunciation between himself and the world, and struggle between himself and his lower self. End of quotation. I say: the gnostic of God unites these qualities; he is therefore the one firmly grounded in knowledge, as stated above.

Theology · Mercy, steadfastness and the promised gathering

In their supplication to God they also say: “Our Lord, do not cause our hearts to deviate” from the way of truth by inclining to follow desire, “after You have guided us” to the path of arrival in Your Presence. “Grant us mercy from Your presence,” gathering our hearts through You and joining our spirits to the witnessing of Your oneness. “You are indeed the Bestower”: You give the hopeful more than he hopes. “Our Lord, You will gather humankind for a day” of recompense “of which there is no doubt.” Gather us, then, with those brought near, for You “do not fail the promise.” Fulfil for us what You have promised on that day. Failure to fulfil a promise is impossible for Him, exalted is He. There is no difficulty concerning the promise of good. As for a threat of evil, if it concerns a specified individual, He does not fail to fulfil it; if it is general, He may forgo it through pardon. And God, exalted is He, knows best.[A012] The Nawādir also says: when those firmly grounded in knowledge referred knowledge of the ambiguous to the One who knows it, saying, “We believe in it; all is from our Lord,” they feared the souls’ avid appetite for seeking it. Knowledge is pleasurable, and the temptation of that pleasure incurs reproach. They therefore fled to their Lord, saying, “Our Lord, do not cause our hearts to deviate after You have guided us. Grant us mercy from Your presence,” knowing that mercy extinguishes that temptation. Since the mystery of predestination will be unveiled on the Day of Resurrection, they yearned towards it, saying, “Our Lord, You will gather humankind ….” They quieted their souls in expectation of that day, when wisdom will be inwardly concealed and power outwardly manifest. End of the passage, rendered in sense.

Spiritual · The understanding of inward visitations

When hearts become pure and repose in the Presence of the Knower of unseen things, divine visitations (الواردات الإلهية), divinely bestowed sciences and holy gifts descend upon them. Some are decisive in construction and clear in meaning; others are indeterminate when first received,

and clarification follows their assimilation: “When We recite it, follow its recitation; then its explanation rests upon Us” [75:18–19]. They may also lie beyond the intellects’ powers of apprehension. Those disposed to deviation and criticism follow what is ambiguous in these visitations, seeking to disturb ordinary people and turn them from the way of the elect, and seeking its interpretation in order to establish the Sacred Law’s case against it. “None knows its interpretation but God,” or one whose effacement in God has been realized: these are those firmly grounded in the knowledge of God. They say, “We believe in it; all is from our Lord,” for hearts purified of desire do not speak from desire. These are the masters of hearts. They say: “Our Lord, do not cause our hearts to deviate” from Your holy Presence “after You have guided us” to arrival there. “Grant us mercy from Your presence” to protect us from looking towards other than You. “You are indeed the Bestower.”[A010] “Our Lord, You will gather humankind”—those journeying towards You—“for a day” whose attainment admits no doubt: the day of meeting. “You do not fail the promise.” Join us to You, then, and interpose between us and whatever cuts us off from You. “You have power over all things.”

Transition ·

He then threatens the people of deviation and corruption, saying:

هُوَ الَّذِي أَنْزَلَ عَلَيْكَ الْكِتابَ مِنْهُ آياتٌ مُحْكَماتٌ هُنَّ أُمُّ الْكِتابِ وَأُخَرُ مُتَشابِهاتٌ فَأَمَّا الَّذِينَ فِي قُلُوبِهِمْ زَيْغٌ فَيَتَّبِعُونَ ما تَشابَهَ مِنْهُ ابْتِغاءَ الْفِتْنَةِ وَابْتِغاءَ تَأْوِيلِهِ وَما يَعْلَمُ تَأْوِيلَهُ إِلاَّ اللَّهُ وَالرَّاسِخُونَ فِي الْعِلْمِ يَقُولُونَ آمَنَّا بِهِ كُلٌّ مِنْ عِنْدِ رَبِّنا وَما يَذَّكَّرُ إِلاَّ أُولُوا الْأَلْبابِ (7) رَبَّنا لا تُزِغْ قُلُوبَنا بَعْدَ إِذْ هَدَيْتَنا وَهَبْ لَنا مِنْ لَدُنْكَ رَحْمَةً إِنَّكَ أَنْتَ الْوَهَّابُ (8) رَبَّنا إِنَّكَ جامِعُ النَّاسِ لِيَوْمٍ لا رَيْبَ فِيهِ إِنَّ اللَّهَ لا يُخْلِفُ الْمِيعادَ (9) قلت: (منه) : خبر مقدم، و (آيات) : مبتدأ، فيوقف على (الكتاب) ، وقيل: (منه) : نعت لكتاب، وهو بعيد. قال ابن السبكي: المحكَم: المتضح المعنى، والمتشابه: ما استأثر الله بعلمه، وقد يُطْلعُ عليه بعضَ أصفيائه. و (هن أم الكتاب) : جملة، وحق الخبر المطابقة فيقول: أمهات، وإنما أفرده على تأويل كل واحدة، أو على أن الكل بمنزلة آية واحدة. والزيغ: الميل عن الحق. و (الراسخون في العلم) : معطوف على (الله) ، أو مبتدأ إن فسر المتشابه بما استأثر الله بعلمه، كمدة بقاء الدنيا ووقت قيام الساعة، أو بما دلّ القاطع على أن ظاهره غير مراد. قاله البيضاوي. و (إذ هديتنا) : ظرف مجرور بالإضافة مسبوك بالمصدر، أي: بعد هدايتك إيانا. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: إن الذي انفرد بالوحدانية والقيومية، ولا يخفى عليه شيء في العالم العلوي والسفلي هُوَ الَّذِي أَنْزَلَ عَلَيْكَ الْكِتابَ المبين، فمنه ما هو آياتٌ مُحْكَماتٌ واضحات المعنى، لا اشتباه فيها ولا إجمال، هُنَّ أُمُّ الْكِتابِ أي: أصله، يرد إليها غيرها، وَمنه آيات أُخَرُ مُتَشابِهاتٌ أي: محتملات، لا يتضح مقصودها لإجمال أو مخالفة ظاهر إلا بالفحص وجودة الفكر، ليظهر فضل العلماء النُقاد، ويزداد حرصهم على الاجتهاد في تَدبُرها وتحصيل العلوم المتوقف عليها استنباط المراد بها، فينال بها، وبإتعاب القرائح في استخراج معانيها، والتوفيق بينها وبين المحكمات، أعلى الدرجات وأرفع المقامات. قال في نوادر الأصول: لمّا تكلم على المتشابه قسّمه على قسمين منه ما طوى علمه إلاَّ على الخواص كعلم فواتح السور، ومنه ما لم يصل إليه أحد من الرسل فمَنْ دُونَهم، وهو سر القدر لا يستقيم لهم مع العبودية، ولو كُشِفَ لفسدت العبودية، فطواه عن الرّسل والملائكة لأنهم في العبودية، فإذا زالت العبودية احتملوها أي: أسرار القدر. هـ. ولمثل هذا يشير قول سهل: للألوهية سر- لو انكشف لبطلت النبوة، وللنبوة سر- لو انكشف لبطل العِلم، وللعلم سر لو انكشف لبطلت الأحكام. هـ. قلت: فَتَحَصَّل أن الكتاب العزيز مشتمل على المحكم والمتشابه. وأما قوله تعالى: كِتابٌ أُحْكِمَتْ آياتُهُ فمعناه: أنها حُفظت من فساد المعنى وركاكة اللفظ، وقوله تعالى: كِتاباً مُتَشابِهاً معناه: أنه يُشبه بعضُه بعضاً في صحة المعنى وجزالة اللفظ. ثم إن الناس في شأن المتشابه على قسمين: فَأَمَّا الَّذِينَ فِي قُلُوبِهِمْ زَيْغٌ: أي: شك، أو ميل عن الحق، كالمبتدعة وأشباههم، فَيَتَّبِعُونَ ما تَشابَهَ مِنْهُ، فيتعلقون بظاهره، أو بتأويل باطل، ابْتِغاءَ الْفِتْنَةِ أي: طلباً لفتنة الناس عن دينهم: بالتشكيك والتلبيس، ومناقضة المحكم بالمتشابه، وَابْتِغاءَ تَأْوِيلِهِ على ما يشتهون ليوافق بدعتهم. روى عن عائشة- رضي الله عنها-: أن النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم- قرأ هذه الآية فقال: «إِذَا رَأَيتُم الذِينَ يَسألُون عن المتشابه منه، ويجادلون فيه، فهم الذين عَنَا الله تعالى، فاحذروهم، ولا تجالسوهم» . (وما يعلم تأويله) على الحقيقة (إلا الله) تعالى، وقد يُطلع عليه بعضَ خواص أوليائه، وهم (الراسخون) أي: الثابتون في العلم، وهم العارفون بالله أهل الفناء والبقاء، وهم أهل التوحيد الخاص ... فقد أطلعهم تعالى على أسرار غيبه، فلم يبق عندهم متشابه في الكتاب ولا في السنة، حال كونهم (يقولون آمنا به) ، وصدقنا أنه من كلامه، (كُلّ من عند ربنا) المحكم والمتشابه، وقد فهمنا مراده في القسمين، وهم أولو الألباب، ولذلك مدحهم فقال: (وما يذكر إلا أولوا الألباب) أي: القلوب الصافية من ظلمة الهوى وغَبَش الحس. سئل عليه الصلاة والسلام: مَن الراسخون في العلم؟ فقال: «من برَّ يمينُه، وصدق لسانُه، واستقام قلبُه، وعفَّ بطنُه وفرجه، فذلك الراسخ في العلم» . وقال نافع بن يزيد: الراسخون في العلم: المتواضعون لله، المتذللون في طلب مرضات الله، لا يتعظمون على مَنْ فوقهم، ولا يحقرون من دونهم. هـ. وقيل: الراسخ في العلم: من وجُد فيه أربعة أشياء: التقوى بينه وبين الله، والتواضع بينه وبين الخلق، والزهد بينه وبين الدنيا، والمجاهدة بينه وبين نفسه. هـ. قلت: ويجمع هذه الأوصاف العارف بالله، فهو الراسخ في العلم كما تقدم. ويقولون أيضاً في تضرعهم إلى الله: رَبَّنا لا تُزِغْ قُلُوبَنا عن نهج الحق بالميل إلى اتباع الهوى، بَعْدَ إِذْ هَدَيْتَنا إلى طريق الوصول إلى حضرتك، وَهَبْ لَنا مِنْ لَدُنْكَ رَحْمَةً تجمع قلوبنا بك، وتضم أرواحنا إلى مشاهدة وحدانيتك، إِنَّكَ أَنْتَ الْوَهَّابُ تهب للمؤمل فوق ما يؤمل. رَبَّنا إِنَّكَ جامِعُ النَّاسِ لِيَوْمٍ الجزاء الذي لا رَيْبَ فِيهِ، فاجمعنا مع المقربين إنك لا تخلف الميعاد، فأنجز لنا ما وعدتنا في ذلك اليوم. وخلف الوعد في حقه تعالى مُحال. أما الوعد بالخير فلا إشكال، وأما الوعيد بالشر، فإن كان في مُعَيِّنٍ فلا يخلفه، وإن كان في الجملة فيخلفه بالعفو. والله تعالى أعلم. وقال في النوادر أيضاً: لَمَّا رَدَّ الراسخون في العلم عِلْمَ المتشابه إلى عالمه، حيث قالوا: آمَنَّا بِهِ كُلٌّ مِنْ عِنْدِ رَبِّنا، خافوا شَرَه النفوس لطلبها فإنَّ العلم لذيذ، وفتنة تلك اللذة لها عتاب، ففزعوا إلى ربهم فقالوا: رَبَّنا لا تُزِغْ قُلُوبَنا بَعْدَ إِذْ هَدَيْتَنا وَهَبْ لَنا مِنْ لَدُنْكَ رَحْمَةً، علموا أن الرحمة تطفئ تلك الفتنة. ولما كان يوم القيامة ينكشف فيه سر القدر حنوا إليه فقالوا: رَبَّنا إِنَّكَ جامِعُ النَّاسِ ... الآية. سكنوا نفوسهم لمجيء ذلك اليوم الذي تَبْطُنِ فيه الحكمة، وتظهر فيه القدرة. هـ. بالمعنى. الإشارة: إذا صفت القلوب، وسكنت في حضرة علاّم الغيوب، تنزلت عليها الواردات الإلهية والعلوم اللدنية، والمواهب القدسية، فمنها ما تكون محكمات المبنى، واضحات المعنى، ومنها ما تكون مجملة في حال ورودها، وبعد الوعي يكون البيان، فَإِذا قَرَأْناهُ فَاتَّبِعْ قُرْآنَهُ ثُمَّ إِنَّ عَلَيْنا بَيانَهُ . وقد تكون خارجة عن مدارك العقول. فأما أهل الزيغ والانتقاد فيتبعون المتشابه من تلك الواردات، ابتغاء فتنة العامة، وصرفهم عن طريق الخاصة، وابتغاء تأويله، ليقيم عليه حجة الشريعة، (وما يعلم تأويله إلا الله) ، أو من تحقق فناؤه في الله، وهم الراسخون في معرفة الله، يقولون: (آمنا به كل من عند ربنا) إذ القلوب المطهرة من الهوى لا تنطق عن الهوى، وهم أرباب القلوب يقولون: (ربنا لا تزغ قلوبنا) عن حضرة قدسك (بعد إذ هديتنا) إلى الوصول إليها، (وهب لنا من لدنك رحمة) تعصمنا من النظر إلى سواك، (إنك أنت الوهاب) ربنا إنك جامع الناس. وهم السائرون إليك ليوم لا ريب في الوصول إليه، وهو يوم اللقاء، (إنَّكَ لا تُخْلفُ المِيعادَ) فاجمع بيننا وبينك، وحل بيننا وبين من يقطعنا عنك (إِنك على كل شيءٍ قدير) . ثم هدد أهل الزيغ والفساد، فقال:

3:10–11 · 3:10–11 · What wealth cannot replace

Cairo I:326–327

10. Those who disbelieve—their wealth and their children will avail them nothing against God. They are the fuel of the Fire. 11. Like the way of Pharaoh’s people and those before them: they denied Our signs, so God seized them for their sins. God is severe in punishment.

Language · Fuel and habitual conduct

I say: waqūd (الوقود), with fatḥa, means firewood; with ḍamma, wuqūd, it is the verbal noun. “Like the way of Pharaoh’s people” is a predicate: their way is like that of Pharaoh’s people. Daʾb (الدأب) is the verbal noun of daʾaba, “to persist”; it was then transferred to mean one’s condition and habitual practice. “They denied” is either a circumstantial clause (حال), with qad understood, a fresh statement explaining their condition, or a predicate if “those before them” begins a new sentence.

Exposition · Neither wealth nor children can ransom them

The Real, glorious is His majesty, says: “Those who disbelieve” in what I have sent down upon our Prophet Muḥammad, blessings and peace be upon him—when they behold punishment, “their wealth and their children will avail them nothing” in place of God’s mercy or obedience to Him, or instead of His punishment. They are the firewood of Hell. Their condition is like that of Pharaoh’s people and those before them: “they denied Our signs, so God seized them for their sins,” destroying them and intensifying their punishment. “God is severe in punishment” towards whoever turns away from Him and relies on another.

Spiritual · No substitute for the Beloved

Whoever rejects the people of spiritual election (أهل الخصوصية), losing his share in witnessing the grandeur of lordship, so that he suffers expulsion and remoteness and misses the companionship of the people of love and affection—neither wealth nor children will replace what he has lost. Sorrows and troubles adhere to him. As the poet says:

Whoever misses communion with You has regret for his portion;
whoever makes You his concern is raised by his aspirations.

Another says:

One who has missed seeking arrival and attaining it
from Him—tell me, what is he seeking?
It is enough for the lover to be effaced from all besides
his Beloved, whether present or absent.

And another:

Everything you part with has a substitute;
but part from God, and there is no substitute for Him.

In the Ḥikam: “What has one found who has lost You? What has one lost who has found You? Disappointed indeed is one content with another as a substitute for You; at a loss indeed is one who seeks to turn away from You.” Everyone who stops with anything other than Him, failing to direct himself towards knowledge of the Master, is in the fire of estrangement and desire, with pharaonic souls and people of low aspiration. We ask God, exalted is He, for well-being.

Transition ·

He then begins reproaching the Jews, having set forth the nature of His Mighty Book and its decisive and ambiguous contents in preparation for addressing them, saying:

إِنَّ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا لَنْ تُغْنِيَ عَنْهُمْ أَمْوالُهُمْ وَلا أَوْلادُهُمْ مِنَ اللَّهِ شَيْئاً وَأُولئِكَ هُمْ وَقُودُ النَّارِ (10) كَدَأْبِ آلِ فِرْعَوْنَ وَالَّذِينَ مِنْ قَبْلِهِمْ كَذَّبُوا بِآياتِنا فَأَخَذَهُمُ اللَّهُ بِذُنُوبِهِمْ وَاللَّهُ شَدِيدُ الْعِقابِ (11) قلت: (الوقود) بالفتح: الحطب، وبالضم: المصدر، (كدأب آل فرعون) خبر، أي: دأبهم كدأب آل فرعون. والدأب: مصدر دأب، إذا دام، ثم نقل إلى الشأن والعادة، و (كذبوا) : حال بإضمار «قد» ، أو مستأنف، تفسير حالهم، أو خبر إن ابتدأت بالذين من قبلهم. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: إِنَّ الَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا بما أنزلته، على نبينا محمد- عليه الصلاة والسلام-، إذا عاينوا العذاب لَنْ تُغْنِيَ عَنْهُمْ أَمْوالُهُمْ وَلا أَوْلادُهُمْ مِنَ اللَّهِ، أي: بدلاً من رحمته أو طاعته، أو بدلاً من عذابه، شَيْئاً، وأولئك هم حطب جهنم، فشأنهم كشأن آلِ فِرْعَوْنَ وَالَّذِينَ مِنْ قَبْلِهِمْ، قد كَذَّبُوا بِآياتِنا فَأَخَذَهُمُ اللَّهُ بِذُنُوبِهِمْ أي: أهلكهم، وشدد العقوبة عليهم، وَاللَّهُ شَدِيدُ الْعِقابِ لمن أعرض عنه وركن إلى غيره. الإشارة: كل من جحد أهل الخصوصية، وفاته حظه من مشاهدة عظمة الربوبية، حتى حصل له الطرد والبعاد، وفاته مرافقة أهل المحبة والوداد، لن تغني عنه- بدلاً مما فاته- أموالُ ولا أولاد، واتصلت به الأحزان والأنكاد كما قال الشاعر: مَنْ فَاتَه منكَ وصلٌ حَظُّه الندمُ ... ومَنْ تَكُنْ هَمِّه تَسْمُو به الهممُ وقال آخر: مَنْ فاتَهُ طَلَبُ الوُصُولِ وَنَيْلُهُ ... مِنْه، فقُلْ: ما الذِي هُوَ يَطلُبُ! حَسْبُ المحِبِّ فناؤه عما سِوى ... مَحْبوبِهِ إنْ حاضِرٌ وَمُغَيَّبُ وقال آخر: لكل شيء إذا فارقْتَهُ عِوَضٌ ... وَلَيْسَ لله إنْ فارقْتَ مِنْ عَوِضِ وفي الحكم: «ماذا وَجَدَ مَنْ فقدك؟ وما الذي فَقَدَ مَنْ وَجَدَك؟ لقد خاب مَنْ رَضِي دونك بدلاً، ولقد خسر من بغى عنك مُتحولاً» . فكل مَن وقف مع شيء من السِّوى، وفاته التوجه إلى معرفة المولى، فهو في نار القطيعة والهوى، مع النفوس الفرعونية، وأهل الهمم الدنية. نسأل الله تعالى العافية. ثم بدأ بعتاب اليهود، بعد أن قرر شأن كتابه العزيز وما اشتمل عليه من المحكم والمتشابه، توطئة للكلام معهم، فقال:

3:12–13 · 3:12–13 · Two armies and the sign of victory

Cairo I:327–328

12. Say to those who disbelieve: “You will be overcome and gathered into Hell—an evil resting-place!” 13. You have already had a sign in two companies that met: one fighting in God’s way, and another unbelieving. They saw them as twice their number, with the very sight of the eye. God strengthens with His help whom He wills. In that is surely a lesson for those possessed of sight.

Exposition · The address at the market of Banū Qaynuqāʿ

I say: when the Messenger of God, blessings and peace be upon him, returned from Badr victorious and helped, with spoils and prisoners, he gathered the Jews in the market of Banū Qaynuqāʿ. He said: “Jews, fear God and embrace Islam. You know that I am truly God’s Messenger. Beware lest God bring down upon you the requital He brought upon Quraysh at Badr.” They said: “Muḥammad, do not be deceived because you encountered inexperienced men with no knowledge of war. If you fight us, you will certainly learn that we are the men.” God then revealed this verse concerning them.[A013]

Exposition · The perceived numbers at Badr

The Real, glorious is His majesty, says: “Say,” Muḥammad, “to those who disbelieve,” among the Children of Israel or generally: “You will be overcome” if you fight the Muslims, “and gathered,” after death and defeat, “into Hell—an evil resting-place”: the punishment you have prepared for yourselves. His promise was fulfilled through the killing of Qurayẓa, the expulsion of Banū l-Naḍīr, the conquest of Khaybar, and the imposition of jizya upon the others. They were overcome wherever encountered and gathered into Hell, except those among them who embraced Islam.[A014] He then urges them to take a lesson from the Muslims’ victory at Badr: “You,” Jews, “have already had a sign”—a manifest lesson and indication of the truth of My saying that you will be overcome—“in two companies” that met on the day of Badr. These were

the Muslims, numbering three hundred and fourteen, and the idolaters, numbering about a thousand. “One company fighting in God’s way” was the believers, “and another unbelieving” was the idolaters. In the reading with tāʾ, “you see them as twice their number,” you Jews see the unbelievers as twice the Muslims’ number, with certain perception. Nevertheless God strengthened the Muslims with victory and reinforcement until He made them prevail over their enemy. He will do the same for them against you. On this interpretation, “seeing” means knowing.[A015] In the reading with yāʾ, the pronoun may refer to the unbelievers: the unbelievers saw the Muslims as twice their own number. This occurred after God had made the Muslims appear few in their eyes, so that they dared to advance upon them. When they met them, however, the Muslims appeared numerous in their eyes until they were defeated: this was reinforcement from God for the believers. Alternatively, the believers saw the idolaters as twice the believers’ number, though they were three times as many, so that they might stand firm against them and be certain of the victory God had promised in His words, “If there are twenty steadfast among you, they will overcome two hundred …” [8:65]. “God strengthens”—fortifies—“with His help whom He wills” to help, as He strengthened the people of Badr. “In that is surely a lesson for those possessed of sight,” whose eyes are open: God helped a people with neither numbers nor equipment against a people possessing both, neither of which availed them anything against God.

Spiritual · The armies of light and otherness

When the heart turns towards its Master, two armies present themselves to it: the army of lights (جند الأنوار), which is the heart’s army, and the army of otherness (جند الأغيار), which is the lower self’s army. Battle joins between them. The army of lights seeks to raise the spirit to its homeland, the Presence of mysteries; the army of otherness seeks to bring the lower self down to the earth of pleasures and appetites and confine it in the prison of created things. When God wills a servant’s happiness, He strengthens for him the army of lights and weakens the army of otherness. The latter is routed and the army of lights takes possession of his heart. Lights continue to descend upon him until the lights of face-to-face encounter shine upon him. He enters the Presence of mysteries—the Presence of witnessing—and finds protection in the neighbourhood of the Loving King. The voices of unseen callers address him: “Gnostic, say to those who disbelieve”—the army of otherness—“You will be overcome and gathered into Hell—an evil resting-place!” When God wills, in His justice, to abandon a servant to himself, He cuts off the reinforcement of lights and strengthens before him the army of otherness. The darkness of the lower self overcomes the heart’s light, confining it in the prison of created things and imprisoning it in the darkness of the human frame. “God strengthens with His help whom He wills.” Thus the meeting of the armies of light and otherness contains a lesson for those possessed of sight.

Transition ·

The Real, exalted is He, then explains the reinforcement of the army of otherness, which prevents eyes from taking heed, saying:

قُلْ لِلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا سَتُغْلَبُونَ وَتُحْشَرُونَ إِلى جَهَنَّمَ وَبِئْسَ الْمِهادُ (12) قَدْ كانَ لَكُمْ آيَةٌ فِي فِئَتَيْنِ الْتَقَتا فِئَةٌ تُقاتِلُ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ وَأُخْرى كافِرَةٌ يَرَوْنَهُمْ مِثْلَيْهِمْ رَأْيَ الْعَيْنِ وَاللَّهُ يُؤَيِّدُ بِنَصْرِهِ مَنْ يَشاءُ إِنَّ فِي ذلِكَ لَعِبْرَةً لِأُولِي الْأَبْصارِ (13) قلت: لمَّا رجع رسول الله صلى الله عليه وسلم من غزوة بدر غالباً منصوراً بالغنائم والأسارى، جمع اليهود في سوق بني قينقاع، وقال لهم: يا معشر اليهود، اتقوا الله وأسلموا، فإنكم تعلمون أني رسولُ الله حقا، واحذروا أن يُنزل الله بكم من نقمته ما أنزل على قريش يوم بدر، فقالوا: يا محمد، لا يغرّنّك أنك لقيت أغماراً لا علم لهم بالحرب، لئن قاتلتنا لتعلَمنَّ أنَّا نحن الناس. فأنزل الله فيهم هذه الآية. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: قُلْ يا محمد لِلَّذِينَ كَفَرُوا من بني إسرائيل، أو مطلقاً: سَتُغْلَبُونَ إن قاتلتم المسلمين، وَتُحْشَرُونَ بعد الموت والهزيمة إِلى جَهَنَّمَ وَبِئْسَ الْمِهادُ ما مهدتم لأنفسكم من العذاب، وقد صدق وعده بقتل قريظة، وإجلاء بني النضير، وفتح خيبر، وضرب الجزية على من عداهم. فقد غُلِبوا أينما ثُقفوا، وحشروا إلى جهنم، إلا من أسلم منهم. ثم ندبهم للاعتبار بما وقع من النصر للمسلمين يوم بدر فقال لهم: قَدْ كانَ لَكُمْ يا معشر اليهود، آيَةٌ أي: عبرة ظاهرة، ودلالة على صدق ما أقول لكم: إنكم ستغلبون، فِي فِئَتَيْنِ أي: جماعتين الْتَقَتا يوم بدر، وهم المسلمون، وكانوا ثلاثمائة وأربعة عشر، والمشركون كانوا زهاء ألف، فِئَةٌ تُقاتِلُ فِي سَبِيلِ اللَّهِ وهم المؤمنون، وَأُخْرى كافِرَةٌ، وهم المشركون، ترونهم مثليهم أي: ترون، يا معشر اليهود، الكفارَ مثلي عدد المسلمين رأي تحقيق، ومع ذلك أيدهم الله بالنصر والمدد حتى نصرهم على عدوهم، وكذلك يفعل بهم معكم. والرؤية، على هذا، علمية. ومن قرأ (بالياء) يكون الضمير راجعاً للكفار، أي: يرى الكفارُ المسلمين مثليهم، وذلك بعد أن قللهم الله في أعينهم حتى اجترءوا عليهم، وتوجهوا إليهم، فلما لاقوهم كثروا في أعينهم حتى غلبوا، مدداً من الله للمؤمنين. أو: يرى المؤمنون المشركين مثلي المؤمنين، وكانوا ثلاثة أمثالهم، ليثبتوا لهم، ويتيقنوا بالنصر الذي وعدهم الله بقوله: إِنْ يَكُنْ مِنْكُمْ عِشْرُونَ صابِرُونَ يَغْلِبُوا مِائَتَيْنِ ... الآية. وَاللَّهُ يُؤَيِّدُ أي: يقوي بِنَصْرِهِ مَنْ يَشاءُ نصره، كما أيد أهل بدر، إِنَّ فِي ذلِكَ لَعِبْرَةً لِأُولِي الْأَبْصارِ المفتوحة. وذلك حين نصر الله قوماً لا عدد لهم ولا عدة، على قوم لهم عدد وعدة، فلم تغن عنهم من الله شيئاً. الإشارة: إذا توجه القلب إلى مولاه تعرض له جندان، أحدهما: جند الأنوار، وهو جند القلب، والثاني: جند الأغيار، وهو جند النفس، فيلتحم بينهما القتال، فجند الأنوار يريد أن يرتقي بالروح إلى وطنها وهو حضرة الأسرار، وجند الأغيار يريد أن يهبط بالنفس إلى أرض الحظوظ والشهوات، فيحبسها في سجن الأكوان، فإذا أراد الله تعالى سعادة عبد، قوي له جند الأنوار، وضَعَّفَ عنه جند الأغيار، فينهزم عنه جند الأغيار، ويستولي على قلبه جند الأنوار، فلا تزال الأنوار تتوارد عليه حتى تشرق عليه أنوار المواجهة، فيدخل حضرة الأسرار، وهي حضرة الشهود، ويتحصن في جوار الملك الودود، وتناديه ألسنة الهواتف: أيها العارف، قل للذين كفروا، وهم جند الأغيار: ستغلبون وتحشرون إلى جهنم وبئس المهاد. وإذا أراد الله خذلان عبده، بعدله، قطع عنه مدد الأنوار، وقوي لديه جند الأغيار، فتستولي ظلمة النفس على نور القلب، فتحبسه في سجن الأكوان، وتسجنه في ظلمة هيكل الإنسان، (والله يويد بنصره من يشاء) . ففي التقاء جندي الأنوار والأغيار عبرة لأولى الأبصار. ثم بين الحق تعالى مدد جند الأغيار، والذي منع الأبصار من الاعتبار، فقال:

3:14 · 3:14 · Desires and their proper limit

Cairo I:328–331

14. Adorned for humankind is love of desires: women, sons, heaped-up stores of gold and silver, marked horses, livestock and cultivated land. That is the enjoyment of the life of this world; with God is the finest place of return.

Language · Adornment, wealth and its names

I say: “Adorned” (زُيّن) has its agent omitted. In reality (حقيقة), the agent is God, for there is no agent but He; according to the Sacred Law (شريعة), it is Satan, who serves as the cloth upon which the filth of impurities is wiped.[A016] A qinṭār is abundant wealth. It has been said to be a hundred thousand dinars, or the contents of a bull’s hide. It is related from the Prophet, blessings and peace be upon him, that he said, “A qinṭār is a thousand dinars”; another version says, “two thousand dinars.” In our usage today it is a thousand mithqāls.[A017] “Heaped-up” (المقنطرة) means arranged in piles one above another. Gold is called dhahab (ذهب) because it goes away and perishes, or because it carries hearts away from the Presence of unseen things. Silver is called fiḍḍa (فضة) because it disperses, or because it disperses the hearts of those occupied with it. “Marked” (المسوّمة) means bearing marks, grazing, or finely formed and beautiful.

[Translator’s print-source notes: [R07-12]]

Exposition · Desire that distracts, and enjoyment received with permission

The Real, glorious is His majesty, says: “Adorned for humankind is love of desires,” and reliance upon familiar pleasures, until this turns them from reflection and taking heed, or from witnessing and inward perception. This concerns those who stop at their enjoyment and are deceived by the appetite for their pleasure. Those reminded by them of the bliss of the Gardens and helped by them to obey the Requiting King do not stop at their enjoyment or turn towards their immediate pleasure. Rather, they descend to them with permission and firm establishment, grounded in certainty. The verse’s warning does not encompass them, in view of the Prophet’s saying, blessings and peace be upon him, “Three things from your world have been made dear to me ….”[A018] One of God’s friends said: “Every appetite veils the heart from God, except the appetite for intercourse”—meaning lawful intercourse. Al-Wartajbī says: “He tries them so that the truthful one, who leaves these appetites, may be distinguished from the false one, who embarks upon seeking them.” It has been said: whoever occupies himself with these things is cut off by them from the path of the Real. Whoever regards them as small and turns from them receives, in exchange, safety from them, and the way to the realities is opened to him. End of quotation.

Exposition · Women, children and the danger of attachment

He begins with the foremost of the desires, “women.” This concerns one captivated by them and thereby turned from God’s remembrance, or one who approaches them unlawfully. It is reported from the Prophet, blessings and peace be upon him: “I have left after me no temptation more harmful to men than women.” Another report says: “Looking at a woman’s beauties is one of Satan’s arrows.” Thus they are represented in the Qurʾān as the very objects of appetite: “Adorned for humankind is love of desires: women ….”[A019] One of the gnostics said: “Whenever Satan has despaired of a person, he has approached him by way of women.”[A020] ʿAlī—God be pleased with him—is quoted as saying: “People, do not obey women’s commands or let them manage an affair of livelihood. If they are left to do as they wish, they ruin the domain and disobey its master. We have found them without religion in their seclusion and without scruple in their appetites. Pleasure with them is little; perplexity through them is great. Their righteous ones are sinful, their wicked ones promiscuous”—that is, adulteresses—“and those protected from fault are nonexistent. They complain of injustice while

being unjust; they withhold themselves while desirous; they swear oaths while lying. Seek God’s protection from their wicked ones, and be apprehensive of their good ones. Peace.” End of quotation.[A021] “And sons”: the Prophet, blessings and peace be upon him, said: “They are indeed the fruit of hearts and the delight of eyes, yet they are also a cause of cowardice, miserliness and sorrow.”[A022]

Exposition · Horses, livestock and cultivation

“And heaped-up stores”—collected and piled—“of gold and silver; and marked horses”: those with distinguishing marks, such as piebald horses, or others. It is reported from the Prophet, blessings and peace be upon him: “Good is tied to horses’ forelocks until the Day of Resurrection: reward and spoils.” Anas said: “Nothing after women was dearer to the Prophet, blessings and peace be upon him, than horses.”[A023] Abū Wahb al-Jushamī reports the Prophet’s words: “Keep horses tethered; stroke their forelocks and put neck-bands upon them, but do not neck-band them with bowstrings. Choose a bay with a white forehead and white lower legs, or a chestnut with a white forehead and white lower legs, or a black horse with a white forehead and white lower legs.” Khabbāb—God be pleased with him—reports: “Horses are of three kinds: a horse for the All-Merciful, a horse for a human being, and a horse for Satan. The horse for the All-Merciful is kept for God, in God’s way, and used to fight God’s enemies. The horse for a human being is ridden upon the road”—in going about one’s needs. “The horse for Satan is staked in betting and gambling.” There is material in al-Bukhārī supporting this.[A024] Also adorned for people is love of “livestock”—camels, cattle, sheep and goats—when it occupies them away from God’s remembrance and they withhold God’s due from it; and “cultivated land,” farming and planting. “That” which has been mentioned “is the enjoyment of the life of this world,” perishing and passing away. “With God is the finest place of return”: the abode of permanence, whose bliss does not perish and whose life never ends, for ever and ever.

Spiritual · Even lights and spiritual states can become an appetite

Everything that cuts the heart off from witnessing, or weakens its journey towards the worshipped King, is an appetite (شهوة), whatever it may be: otherness or lights, knowledge or states, or anything else. The women are otherness; the sons, lights; the heaped-up stores of gold, the sciences of the Path; silver, the sciences of the Sacred Law; marked horses, spiritual states; livestock, remembrances; and cultivated land, the exercise of contemplation. Whoever stops with the sweetness of any of these and fails to pass into the repose of witnessing and direct vision has made it an appetite for himself. Having mentioned kinds of desire, the Real, exalted is He, encourages detachment from them: “That is the enjoyment of the life of this world; with God is the finest place of return.” Abū Hāshim al-Zāhid—God be pleased with him—said: “God has marked the world with estrangement, so that the disciple’s intimacy may be with his Lord rather than with it, and the obedient may advance by turning away from it. Those who know God feel estranged from the world and long for God.” End of quotation.

The Prophet, blessings and peace be upon him, sought refuge from the evil of its trial, both its wealth and its poverty. Most of the Qurʾān contains censure of it and warnings against it. Indeed, every caller to God has warned against it and encouraged desire for the hereafter. This is the principal purpose in explaining the revealed laws. How could it be otherwise? The world is God’s enemy, because it cuts off the way of communion with Him; for that reason He has not looked at it since creating it.[A025] It is the enemy of His friends, adorning itself until they have to swallow the bitterness of patience in breaking with it. It is also the enemy of His enemies: it draws them on by its deception and captures them in its net. They trust it, and it abandons them when they most need it. May God suffice us against its evil through His gracious favour and generosity.

Transition ·

The Real, exalted is He, then points to the supremely important aim for anyone of ample intelligence, saying:

زُيِّنَ لِلنَّاسِ حُبُّ الشَّهَواتِ مِنَ النِّساءِ وَالْبَنِينَ وَالْقَناطِيرِ الْمُقَنْطَرَةِ مِنَ الذَّهَبِ وَالْفِضَّةِ وَالْخَيْلِ الْمُسَوَّمَةِ وَالْأَنْعامِ وَالْحَرْثِ ذلِكَ مَتاعُ الْحَياةِ الدُّنْيا وَاللَّهُ عِنْدَهُ حُسْنُ الْمَآبِ (14) قلت: (زُين) : بحذف الفاعل، وهو الله، حقيقة إذ لا فاعل سواه، أو الشيطان، شَريعَةً إذ هو منديل لمسح أوساخ الأقذار. والقنطار: المال الكثير، وقيل: مائة ألف دينار، وقيل: ملء مسك الثور. وروى عنه- عليه الصلاة والسلام- أنه قال: «القنطار: ألف دينار» ، وفي رواية: «ألفا دينار» ، وفي عرفنا اليوم: ألف مثقال. والمقنطرة: المنضدة بعضها فوق بعض، وسمي الذهب ذهباً لذهابه وفنائه، أو لذهابه بالقلوب عن حضرة الغيوب، وسميت الفضة فضة لأنها تَنْفَضُّ أي: تنفرق، أو تفرق القلوب لمن اشتغل بها. والمسوَّمة: المعلمة أو الراعية أو المطهمة الحسان. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: زُيِّنَ لِلنَّاسِ حُبُّ الشَّهَواتِ والركون إلى المألوفات، حتى صرفهم ذلك عن النظر والاعتبار، أو الشهود والاستبصار، وذلك لمن وقف مع متعتها، وغرته شهوة لذتها، وأما من ذكرته نعيم الجنان، وأعانته على طاعة الملك الديان، فلم يقف مع متعتها، ولا التفت إلى عاجل شهوتها، بل نزل إليها بالإذن والتمكين، والرسوخ في اليقين، فلا يشمله تحذير الآية لقوله- عليه الصلاة والسلام-: «حبب إلي من دنياكم ثلاث ... » الحديث. وقال بعض الأولياء: [كل شهوة تحجب القلب عن الله، إلا شهوة الجماع] يعني الحلال، وقال الورتجبي: ابتلاهم حتى يظهر الصادق بترك هذه الشهوات، من الكاذب بالشروع في طلبها، قيل: من اشتغل بهذه الأشياء قطعته عن طريق الحق، ومن استصغرها وأعرض عنها، عوض عليها السلامة منها، وفتح له الطريق إلى الحقائق. هـ. ثم بدأ برأس الشهوات فقال: مِنَ النِّساءِ وذلك لمن شُغف بهن فصرف عن ذكر الله، أو تناولهن على وجه الحرام. وفي الخبر عنه- عليه الصلاة والسلام-: «مَا تَرَكْتُ في الناس بَعْدِي فِتْنَةً أَضَرُّ عَلَى الرِّجَالِ مِنَ النِّسَاءِ» . وفي خبر آخر: «النظر إلى محاسن المرأة من سهام إبليس» . ومَنْ ثَمَّ جُعِلْنَ في القرآن عين الشهوات، قال تعالى: زُيِّنَ لِلنَّاسِ حُبُّ الشَّهَواتِ مِنَ النِّساءِ. وقال بعض العارفين: ما أيس الشيطانُ من إنسان قط إلا أتاه من قبل النساء. وقال على رضى الله عنه: أيها الناس، لا تطيعوا للنساء أمراً، ولا تَدَعُوهُنَّ يدبرن أمر عيش، فإنهن إن تُرِكْنَ وما يُرِدْن أفسدن الملك، وعصَين المالك، وجدناهن لا دين لهن في خلَواتهن، ولا ورع لهن عند شهواتهن، اللذة بهن يسيرة، والحيرة بهن كثيرة، فأما صوالحهن ففاجرات، وأما طوالحهن فعاهرات- أي: زانيات-، وأما المعصومات فهن المعدومات، يتظلمن وهن الظالمات، ويتمنعن وهن الراغبات، ويحلفن وهن الكاذبات، فاستعيذوا بالله من شرارهن، وكونوا على وجل من خيارهن، والسلام(1). هـ. وَالْبَنِينَ: قال- عليه الصلاة والسلام-: «إنهم لثمرة القلوب، وقُرَّةُ الأعيُن، وإنهم مع ذلك لَمَجْبَنَةٌ مَبْخَلَةٌ مَحْزَنَةٌ» . وَالْقَناطِيرِ الْمُقَنْطَرَةِ: أي: المجموعة المنضدة، مِنَ الذَّهَبِ وَالْفِضَّةِ. وَالْخَيْلِ الْمُسَوَّمَةِ أي: المعلمة: وهي البلق، أو غيرها، وفي الحديث عنه صلّى الله عليه وسلم: «الخَيْلُ معقودٌ في نَواصِيها الخير إلى يوم القِيَامةِ، الأجرُ والمَغْنمُ» . وعن أنس قال: (لم يكن شيء أحب إلى النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم بعد النساء، من الخيل) . وعن أبي وهب الجشمي قال النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: «ارتبطوا الخيل، وامسحوا بنواصيها، وقَلِّدُوها، ولا تقلدوها الأوتار، وعليكم بكل كُمَيْت(2) أغر مُحَجِّلِ، أو أشقر أغر محجل، أو أدهم أغر محجّل» . وعن خباب رضي الله عنه قال: قال رسول الله صلّى الله عليه وسلم: «الخيل ثلاثة: فرس للرحمن، وفرس للإنسان، وفرس للشيطان، فأما فرس الرحمن فما اتخذ لله في سبيل الله، وقوتل عليه أعداء الله، وأما فرس الإنسان فما استطرق عليه- أي: ركب عليه في طريق حوائجه، وأما فرس الشيطان فما روهن عليه، وقومر عليه» . وفي البخاري ما يشهد لهذا. ومما زُيِّن للناس أيضاً: حب الْأَنْعامِ، وهي الإبل والبقر والغنم، إن شغلته عن ذكر الله، ومنع منها حق الله، وَالْحَرْثِ أي: الزراعة والغراسة، ذلِكَ الذي ذكرت مَتاعُ الْحَياةِ الدُّنْيا الفانية الزائلة، وَاللَّهُ عِنْدَهُ حُسْنُ الْمَآبِ، أي: المرجع في دار البقاء التي لا يفنى نعيمها، ولا تنقطع حياتها إلى أبد الأبد. الإشارة: كل ما يقطع القلبّ عن الشهود، أوُ يُفَتِّرُهُ عن السير إلى الملك المعبود، فهو شهوة، كائناً ما كان، أغياراً أو أنواراً، أو علوماً أو أحوالاً، أو غير ذلك، فالنساء الأغيار، والبنون الأنوار، والقناطير المقنطرة من الذهب علوم الطريقة، والفضة علوم الشريعة، والخيل المسومة هي الأحوال، والأنعام الأذكار، والحرث استعمال الفكرة. فكل مَنْ وقف مع حلاوة شيء من هذا، ولم يُفْضِ إلى راحة الشهود والعيان، فهي في حقه شهوة. وبعد أن ذكر الحق تعالى أنواعاً من الشهوات، زَهَّدَ فيها فقال: ذلِكَ مَتاعُ الْحَياةِ الدُّنْيا وَاللَّهُ عِنْدَهُ حُسْنُ الْمَآبِ قال أبو هاشم الزاهد رضي الله عنه: وَسَمَ اللهُ الدنيا بالوحشة ليكون أنس المريد بربه دونها، وليقبل المطيعون بالإعراض عنها، وأهلُ المعرفة بالله من الدنيا مستوحشون، وإلى الله مشتاقون. هـ. وقد تعوذ النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم من شر فتنتها، غناها وفقرها. وأكثرُ القرآن مشتملٌ على ذمها، وتحذير الخلق منها، بل ما من داع يدعو إلى الله تعالى إلا وقد حذر منها، ورغَّب في الآخرة، بل هو المقصود بالذات من بيان الشرائع، وكيف لا- وهي عدوة الله لقطعها طريق الوصلة إليه، ولذلك لم ينظر إليها منذ خلقها. وعدوة لأوليائه لأنها تزينت بزينتها حتى تجرعوا مرارة الصبر في مقاطعتها، وعدوة لأعدائه لأنها استدرجتهم بمكرها، واقتنصتهم بشبكتها، فوثقوا بها، فخذلتهم أحوج ما كانوا إليها. كفانا الله شرّها بمنِّه وكرمه. ثم نبه الحق تعالى على ما هو المقصود الأهم لمن له عقل وافر، فقال:

3:15–17 · 3:15–17 · The higher gifts and those who seek them

Cairo I:331–332

15. Say: “Shall I tell you of something better than that? For those who are mindful of God there are, with their Lord, gardens beneath which rivers flow, abiding there, purified spouses, and good pleasure from God.” God sees His servants: 16. those who say, “Our Lord, we believe: forgive us our sins and guard us from the punishment of the Fire”; 17. the steadfast, the truthful, the devoutly obedient, those who spend, and those who seek forgiveness before dawn.

Language · The promise and its recipients

I say: “For those” is the predicate, and “gardens” the subject. This begins a new statement explaining the superiority. Riḍwān (الرضوان) has two pronunciations, with ḍamma or kasra, like ʿudwān and ṭughyān. “Those who say” is an apposition (بدل) to “those who are mindful of God,” a predicate of an understood subject, an accusative of praise (المدح), or an apposition to “the servants.” “The steadfast” and what follows qualify the relative expression.

Exposition · From worldly enjoyment to divine good pleasure

The Real, glorious is His majesty, says: “Say,” Muḥammad, “Shall I tell you of something better” than the perishing desires and passing pleasures I have mentioned? It is what God has prepared for the godfearing when they meet their Lord: “gardens beneath” whose palaces “rivers flow”—water, milk, honey and wine—“abiding there,” unlike the perishing bliss of this world. They have “spouses” from among the wide-eyed maidens, purified of menstruation, childbirth bleeding and all that is repellent, “and good pleasure from God,” the greatest of blessings. Consider how the Real, glorious is His majesty, mentions the lowest, middle and highest bliss. The lowest is worldly enjoyment, adorned for people; the middle, the bliss of the Gardens; the highest, the good pleasure of the All-Merciful. In the sound report from the Prophet, blessings and peace be upon him: “God says to the people of the Garden, ‘People of the Garden!’ They answer, ‘At Your service, our Lord, and gladly; good is in Your hands.’ He says, ‘Are you satisfied?’ They say, ‘How should we not be satisfied, when You have given us what You have given none of the worlds?’ He says, ‘Shall I give you something better than that?’ They say, ‘Our Lord, what could be better than that?’ He says, ‘I shall cause My good pleasure to rest upon you, and shall never be angry with you thereafter.’”[A026]

“God sees His servants”: none of their deeds is hidden from Him, and He rewards the one who does well and punishes the one who does ill; or He sees the conditions of the godfearing, “those who say, ‘Our Lord, we believe: forgive us our sins and guard us from the punishment of the Fire.’” Basing the request upon faith alone indicates that faith suffices to merit forgiveness and to make one ready for it.

Exposition · Truthfulness and the hours before dawn

He then describes the godfearing as “the steadfast”: in performing commands, avoiding prohibitions, and bearing deprivation, adversity and the time of battle. “The truthful” in words, deeds and states, whose secret and public life agree. “The devoutly obedient”; “those who spend” their wealth in God’s way; and “those who seek forgiveness before dawn.” Supplication then is nearer to acceptance, because worship at that time is harder, the soul clearer and the spirit more collected, especially for those who keep night vigil. It is said that they used to pray until the time before dawn and then seek forgiveness and supplicate. The Prophet, blessings and peace be upon him, is reported to have said: “God, exalted is He, says, ‘I am about to punish the earth’s people; but when I look upon those who frequent My houses, those who keep night vigil, those who love one another for My sake, and those who seek forgiveness before dawn, I turn punishment away from them.’”[A027] Sufyān said: “God has a wind called the Cry, which blows before dawn, carrying remembrance and pleas for forgiveness to the Compelling King.” He said: “We have been told that at the beginning of the night a caller cries, ‘Let the devoutly obedient arise!’ They rise and pray until before dawn. Then a caller cries, ‘Where are those who seek forgiveness before dawn?’ Those people seek forgiveness, and others rise and pray, joining them. When dawn breaks a caller cries, ‘Let the heedless arise!’ They rise from their beds like the dead raised from their graves.”[A027]

Spiritual · The Gardens of recognition

For those who guard against witnessing anything other than God there are, with their Lord, gardens of recognition, beneath which flow rivers of knowledge and varieties of wisdom, purified of defects and free from flaws. The breeze of good pleasure blows upon them, bearing repose and fragrance. They abide in the bliss of witnessing and direct vision. God sees His sincere servants, purified of faults and cleansed of the stain of sins: steadfast in continual struggle, truthful in seeking witnessing, obedient to the rulings of servanthood, expending themselves and their lifeblood in seeking to witness the lights of lordship, and asking forgiveness for witnessing otherness—especially when the breeze before dawn blows. For many worshippers and ascetics have been occupied by the sweetness of that breeze away from contemplating the mysteries of the Compeller: the mysteries of unity to which He points by saying:

قُلْ أَأُنَبِّئُكُمْ بِخَيْرٍ مِنْ ذلِكُمْ لِلَّذِينَ اتَّقَوْا عِنْدَ رَبِّهِمْ جَنَّاتٌ تَجْرِي مِنْ تَحْتِهَا الْأَنْهارُ خالِدِينَ فِيها وَأَزْواجٌ مُطَهَّرَةٌ وَرِضْوانٌ مِنَ اللَّهِ وَاللَّهُ بَصِيرٌ بِالْعِبادِ (15) الَّذِينَ يَقُولُونَ رَبَّنا إِنَّنا آمَنَّا فَاغْفِرْ لَنا ذُنُوبَنا وَقِنا عَذابَ النَّارِ (16) الصَّابِرِينَ وَالصَّادِقِينَ وَالْقانِتِينَ وَالْمُنْفِقِينَ وَالْمُسْتَغْفِرِينَ بِالْأَسْحارِ (17) قلت: (للذين) : خبر، و (جنات) : مبتدأ، وهو استئناف لبيان الخيرية، والرضوان فيه لغتان: الضم والكسر، كالعدوان والطغيان، و (الذين يقولون) : بدل من (الذين اتقوا) ، أو خبر عن مضمر، أو منصوب على المدح، أو بدل من العباد، و (الصابرين) وما بعده: نعت الموصول. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: قُلْ يا محمد: أأخبركم بِخَيْرٍ من الذي ذكرتُ لكم من الشهوات الفانية واللذات الزائلة، وهو ما أعد الله للمتقين عند لقاء ربهم، وهو جَنَّاتٌ تَجْرِي مِنْ تحت قصورها الأنهارُ من الماء واللبن والعسل والخمر، خالِدِينَ فِيها، لا كنعيم الدنيا الفاني، ولهم فيها أَزْواجٌ من الحور العين، مطهرات من الحيض والنفاس وسائر المستقذرات، وَرِضْوانٌ مِنَ اللَّهِ الذي هو (أكبر) النعم. فانظر: كيف ذكر الحق- جل جلاله- أدنى النعيم وأوسطه وأعلاه؟ فأدناه: متاع الدنيا الذي زُين للناس، وأوسطه: نعيم الجنان، وأعلاه: رضي الرحمن. وفي الحديث الصحيح عنه صلى الله عليه وسلم: «يَقُولُ الله تعالى لأهِلْ الْجنَّةِ: يَا أهْلَ الجَنَّةِ، فيقول أهْلَ الجَنَّةِ: لَبْيكَ رَبَّنَا وَسَعْدَيْكِ، والخير في يديك، فيقول: هَلْ رضِيتُم؟ فيقولون: ما لَنَا لا نَرضى وَقَد أعطيتنا ما لم تُعط أَحَداً مِنْ العالمين، فَيَقُولُ: ألا أعْطِيكُم أَفْضَلَ من ذلك؟ فيقُولون: يا ربنا، وأيُّ شَيء أَفْضَلُ مِنْ ذلك؟ قال: أُحل عليكم رضواني فلا أسخط عليكم أبدا» . وَاللَّهُ بَصِيرٌ بِالْعِبادِ لا يخفى عليه شيء من أعمالهم، فيثيب المحسن، ويعاقب المسيء، أو: (بصير) بأحوال المتقين. الَّذِينَ يَقُولُونَ رَبَّنا إِنَّنا آمَنَّا فَاغْفِرْ لَنا ذُنُوبَنا وَقِنا عَذابَ النَّارِ. وفي ترتيب السؤال على مجرد الإيمان دليل على أنه كاف في استحقاق المغفرة والاستعداد لها. ثم وصف المتقين بقوله: الصَّابِرِينَ على أداء الأمر واجتناب النهي، وفي البأساء والضراء وحين البأس، وَالصَّادِقِينَ في أقوالهم وأفعالهم وأحوالهم، فاستوى سرهم وعلانيتهم، وَالْقانِتِينَ أي: المطيعين، وَالْمُنْفِقِينَ أموالهم في سبيل الله، وَالْمُسْتَغْفِرِينَ بِالْأَسْحارِ لأن الدعاء فيها أقرب إلى الإجابة لأن العبادة حينئذٍ أشق، والنفس أصفى، والروح أجمع، وَلاَ سيما للمتهجدين. قيل: إنهم كانوا يُصلُون إلى السحر، ثم يستغفرون ويدعون، وفي الحديث عنه صلّى الله عليه وسلم أنه قال: (إِنَّ اللهَ تعالى يقول: إني لأهُمُّ بأهل الأرض عذاباً، فإذا نظرت إلى عُمَّار بيوتي، وإلى المتهجدين، وإلى المتحابين فِيَّ، وإلى المستغفرين بالأسحار، صرفت عنهم العذاب) . وقال سفيان: إن لله ريحاً يقال لها الصيحة، تهبُّ وقتَ السحر، تحمل الأذكار والاستغفار إلى الملك الجبار. قال: وبلَغنا أنه إذا كان أول الليل، نادى مناد: ألا ليقم القانتون، فيقومون يُصلون إلى السحَر، فإذا كان وقت السحر، ينادي منادٍ: أين المستغفرون بالأسحار؟ فيستغفر أولئك، ويقوم آخرون، ويُصلون، فيلحقون بهم، فإذا طلع الفجر، نادى مناد: ألا ليقم الغافلون، فيقومون من فرشِهم كالموتى إذا نُشروا من قبورهم. الإشارة: للذين اتقوا شهودَ السّوى عند ربهم جنات المعارف، تجري من تحتها أنهار العلوم، وأصناف الحكم، مطهرة من العلل، منزهة من الخلل، تهب عليهم نسيم الرضوان، تحمل الرَّوُحَ والريحان، مخلدون في نعيم الشهود والعيان، والله بصير بعباده المخلصين، المنزَّهين من العيوب، المبرّئين من درن الذنوب، الصابرين على دوام المجاهدة، والصادقين في طلب المشاهدة، والقانتين لأحكام العبودية، والمنفقين أنفسَهُمْ ومُهَجَهم في طلب مشاهدة أنوار الربوبية، والمستغفرين من شهود الأغيار، وخصوصاً إذا هبّ نسيم الأسحار، فإن كثيراً من العباد والزهاد شغلتهم حلاوة نسيم الأسحار عن مطالعة أسرار الجبار، وهى أسرار التوحيد التي أشار إليها بقوله:

3:18 · 3:18 · God’s testimony to His oneness

Cairo I:332–334

18. God bears witness that there is no god but He, as do the angels and those possessed of knowledge, while He upholds justice. There is no god but He, the Mighty, the Wise.

Language · The circumstantial clause and the following affirmation

I say: “Upholding” (قائماً) is a circumstantial qualifier (حال) of “God.” Its restriction to one of the coordinated elements is admissible because there is no confusion, as in “We bestowed upon him Isaac, and Jacob as an additional gift” [21:72]. One cannot say “Zayd and ʿAmr came riding,” with the singular qualifier, without an indication of which is meant. Alternatively, it qualifies “He” (هو), and the sentence governs it, because it is a confirmatory circumstantial qualifier (حال مؤكدة): He alone is God, upholding justice; or it is established of Him while He upholds justice. Qisṭ (القسط) means justice. “Indeed, the religion …” [3:19] is a new sentence confirming the first: no religion is pleasing to God except acknowledgement of the testimony and entering what Muḥammad, blessings and peace be upon him, brought. In the reading with fatḥa, anna, it is apposition to “that He” (أنه): complete apposition (بدل الكل) if Islam is interpreted as faith, or apposition of inclusion (بدل الاشتمال) if it is interpreted as the Sacred Law.[A028]

[Translator’s print-source notes: [R07-13]]

Exposition · Signs, testimony and the honour of knowledge

The Real, glorious is His majesty, says: “God bears witness that there is no god but He”: He makes His oneness clear by setting up indications of it and sending down verses declaring it, or through His wondrous governance, perfected workmanship and well-ordered affairs. Concerning this the poet says:

How astonishing that God should be disobeyed,
or denied by one who denies!
In every movement and every stillness
there is always a witness to God.
In everything He has a sign
indicating that He is One.

One of the Arabs was asked for the proof that the world has a Maker. He replied: “Dung points to a camel and footprints to a traveller. Do not a celestial structure of such subtlety and a lower centre of such density point to the All-Aware Maker?”[A029] “The angels” also bear witness, acknowledging and declaring oneness; and “those possessed of knowledge”—the prophets and those who know God—through believing it and adducing proof for it. In its clarification and disclosure, this is likened to a witness’s testimony. It indicates the honour and excellence of the people of knowledge, since He joins their testimony to His own. Knowledge is God’s exalted attribute and His greatest gift. Scholars are the standards of Islam, the foremost on the way to the Abode of Peace, the lamps of places and the proofs of their times. Jābir reports the Prophet’s words: “An hour in which a scholar reclines upon his bed considering his knowledge is better than a worshipper’s worship for seventy years.” Muʿādh reports: “Learn knowledge: learning it for God is reverent fear; studying it is glorification; investigating it is struggle; teaching it to one who does not know is charity; and recalling it among its people is a means of nearness.” At the end of the report he says of the people of knowledge: “The angels desire their friendship, stroke them with their wings and seek forgiveness for them in their prayers. Every moist and dry thing seeks forgiveness for them: the fish and creatures of the sea, the beasts and livestock of the earths, the heaven and its stars. Knowledge is the life of hearts from blindness, the light of sight from darkness, and the strength of bodies from weakness. It brings the servant to the rank of the free and the companionship of kings. Reflection upon it equals fasting, and studying it equals standing in prayer. Through it the lawful and unlawful are known, and kinship ties maintained. Knowledge is the leader; action follows it. The fortunate are inspired with it, and the wretched deprived of it.”[A030]

The Real, exalted is He, is “upholding justice”: governing His creation with justice in what He decrees and settles. “There is no god but He.” He repeats the testimony for emphasis, further consideration of oneness, and affirmation of it after establishing its proof. Jaʿfar al-Ṣādiq says: “The first is description and affirmation of unity; the second is prescription and instruction”—that is, say, “There is no god but He.” Alternatively, it is repeated to attach to it “the Mighty, the Wise,” making clear that He is described by both. He places “the Mighty” first, so that knowledge of His power precedes knowledge of His wisdom.

شَهِدَ اللَّهُ أَنَّهُ لاَ إِلهَ إِلاَّ هُوَ وَالْمَلائِكَةُ وَأُولُوا الْعِلْمِ قائِماً بِالْقِسْطِ لاَ إِلهَ إِلاَّ هُوَ الْعَزِيزُ الْحَكِيمُ (18) إِنَّ الدِّينَ عِنْدَ اللَّهِ الْإِسْلامُ ... قلت: (قائماً) : حال مِن (الله) ، وإنما جاز من بعض المعطوفات لعدم اللبس، كقوله: وَوَهَبْنا لَهُ إِسْحاقَ وَيَعْقُوبَ نافِلَةً ... ، ولا يجوز: جاء زيد وعمرو راكباً لعدم القرينة، أو مِن (هو) ، والعامل الجملة لأنه حال مؤكدة، أي: تفرد قائماً، أو حقه قائماً، (بالقسط) أي: العدل، و (إن الدين) : جملة مستأنفة مؤكدة للأولى، أي: لا دين مرضى عند الله سوى الإقرار بالشهادة والدخول فيما جاء به محمد صلى الله عليه وسلم، ومن قرأ بالفتح فهو بدل من (أنه) ، بدل الكل، إن فسر الإسلام بالإيمان، وبدل الاشتمال إن فسر بالشريعة. يقول الحق جلّ جلاله: شَهِدَ اللَّهُ أَنَّهُ لاَ إِلهَ إِلَّا هُوَ أي: بيَّن وحدانيتَه بنصب الدلائل الدالة عليها، وإنزال الآيات الناطقة بها، أو بتدبيره العجيب وصنعته المتقنة وأموره المحكمة، وفي ذلك يقول القائل: يَا عَجَباً كيف يُعْصَى الإلهُ ... أم كيف يَجْحَدُه الجاحدُ؟! وللهِ في كل تحريكةٍ ... وتسكينةٍ أبداً شاهدُ وفي كُلِّ شَيءٍ له آيةٌ ... تَدُلُّ على أنَّه واحِدُ(1) وقيل لبعض العرب: ما الدليل على أن للعالم صانعاً؟ فقال: البعرة تدل على البعير، وآثار القدم تدل على المسير، فهيكل علوي بهذه اللطافة، ومركز سفلي بهذه الكثافة، أمَا يدلان على الصانع الخبير؟! وَشهدت الْمَلائِكَةُ أيضاً بالإقرار بالوحدانية والإخبار بها، وَأُولُوا الْعِلْمِ وهم: الأنبياء والعلماء بالله، بالإيمان بها والاحتجاج عليها، شبه ذلك في البيان والكشف بشهادة الشاهد. وفيه دليل شرف أهل العلم وفضلهم، حيث قرن شهادتهم بشهادته لأن العلم صفة الله العليا ونعمته العظمى، والعلماء أعلام الإسلام، والسابقون إلى دار السلام، وسُرج الأمكنة وحجج الأزمنة. وعن جابر قال: قال النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: «ساعة من عالم يتّكىء على فِرَاشِهِ، ينظُرُ في علمهِ، خَيرٌ مِنْ عِبَادَة العَابِد سَبعينَ عاماً» . وعن معاذ قالَ: قال النبي صلى الله عليه وسلم: «تعلموا العلم فإن تعلمه لله خشيةٌ، ومدارستَه تسبيحٌ، والبحث فيه جهادٌ، وتعليمه مَنْ لا يعلمه صدقةٌ، وتذكُّره في أهله قُرْبَة» . ثم قال في آخر الحديث في فضل أهل العلم: «وتَرْغَبُ الملائكة في خُلتِهم، وبأجنحتها تمسحُهم، وفي صلاتها تستغفر لهم، وكلُّ رطب ويابس يستغفر لهم. حتى حيتان البحر وهوامه، وسباع الأرضين وأنعامها، والسماء ونجومها، ألا وإن العلم حياةُ القلوب من العمى، ونورُ الأبصار من الظلم، وقوة الأبدان من الضعف، يبلغ بالعبد منزل الأحرار ومجالسة الملوك، والفكر فيه يُعْدل بالصيام، ومدارسته بالقيام، وبه يُعرف الحلال والحرام، وبه تُوصلَ الأرحام، العلم إمام والعمل تابعه، يُلْهَمُه السعداء، ويحرمه الأشقياء» . حال كون الحقّ تعالى قائِماً بِالْقِسْطِ أي: مُدبراً لأمر خلقه بالعدل، فيما حكم وأبرم، لاَ إِلهَ إِلَّا هُوَ، كرر الشهادة للتأكيد، ومزيد الاعتبار بأمر التوحيد، والحكم به، بعد إقامته الدليل عليه. وقال جعفر الصادق: (الأُولى وصف وتوحيد، والثانية رسم وتعليم) . أي: قولوا: لاَ إِلهَ إِلَّا هُوَ، أو ليرتب عليه قوله: الْعَزِيزُ الْحَكِيمُ، فيعلم أنه الموصوف بهما، وقدَّم «العزيز» ليتقدم العلم بقدرته على العلم بحكمته.

3:19 · 3:19 · Submission, division and reverent disagreement

Cairo I:334–335

19. The religion in God’s sight is al-islām (submission). Those given the Book differed only after knowledge had come to them, through mutual rivalry. Whoever disbelieves in God’s signs—God is swift in reckoning.

Exposition · The religion pleasing to God

“The religion with God is Islam”: the religion pleasing to Him is submission to the affirmation of unity and yielding to the one who brought it. Anas—God be pleased with him—reports the Prophet’s words: “Whoever recites this verse [3:18] when going to sleep, God creates seventy thousand creatures who seek forgiveness for him until the Day of Resurrection.” It is the greatest testimony in God’s Book. “Whoever recites it as far as ‘the Wise,’ then says, ‘I too bear witness to what God has borne witness to, and I entrust this testimony to God: it is a deposit for me with Him,’ the Real, exalted is He, says, ‘This servant of Mine has a covenant with Me, and I am most entitled to fulfil a covenant. Admit My servant to the Garden.’”[A031]

Spiritual · Distinction, gathering and obedience

The opening of the verse [3:18] points to distinction (الفرق), and its conclusion to gathering (الجمع), as is His customary manner in His Mighty Book: He sets forth the Sacred Law first and realization second. The Real, glorious is His majesty, affirms the testimony of angels and people of knowledge alongside His testimony, establishing the mystery of the Sacred Law. He then effaces it with “There is no god but He, the Mighty, the Wise,” by the ruling of Reality. Affirming created outlines (الرسوم) is Sacred Law; effacing them is Reality.[A032] The unification of those concerned with outlines and forms is an indication from behind the veil. The unification of the people of effacement and disappearance is testimony from within the veil. The former is indication and demonstration; the latter testimony and direct vision. People of evidence and demonstration are ordinary people in relation to those of witnessing and direct vision. Affirmation of outlines is Islam and faith; their effacement is witnessing and spiritual excellence (الإحسان). Every affirmation of unity whose fruit fails to appear in the limbs as yielding and submission to the rulings of servanthood is deficient. For He says, “The religion with God is Islam”: outward and inward submission to the rulings of overpowering divine governance and of obligation. Whoever lacks submission lacks complete religion.

Transition ·

He then mentions those for whom abandonment was already decreed despite the radiance of evidence and demonstration, saying:

Qurʾānic continuation · 3:19b
وَمَا اخْتَلَفَ الَّذِينَ أُوتُوا الْكِتابَ إِلاَّ مِنْ بَعْدِ مَا جاءَهُمُ الْعِلْمُ بَغْياً بَيْنَهُمْ وَمَنْ يَكْفُرْ بِآياتِ اللَّهِ فَإِنَّ اللَّهَ سَرِيعُ الْحِسابِ
Those given the Book differed only after knowledge had come to them, through mutual rivalry. Whoever disbelieves in God’s signs—God is swift in reckoning.

Language · The cause of division

I say: “Through rivalry” (بغياً) is an accusative of purpose or cause (مفعول له), giving the cause of the disagreement.

Exposition · Knowledge contradicted by rivalry

The Real, glorious is His majesty, says: the Jews and Christians differed concerning the truth of Islam and adherence to it only after knowledge had come to them: after they had the means to know its soundness and that the religion with God is Islam. They denied it unjustly and enviously. Alternatively, the possessors of earlier books differed concerning Islam—some affirming it, others saying it was restricted to the Arabs, and others denying it altogether—only after knowledge of its truth and the universality of its summons had been established for them. Or they differed concerning unity: Christians asserted a trinity and Jews said, “ʿUzayr is God’s son,” changing what they had soundly known of unity.[A033] Al-Rabīʿ said: “When death approached Moses, peace be upon him, he called seventy learned men of his people and entrusted the Torah to them. After the first, second and third generations had passed, division arose among them: these were ‘those given the Book,’ the descendants of the seventy. They shed blood, and evil and disagreement arose among them.” This was after knowledge—the explanation contained in the Torah—had come to them, “through mutual rivalry”: seeking dominion and leadership and envying one another.
